## Findings and fixes

1. The initial effective backend notes still instructed 480-second six-chapter outlines despite target_minutes=10. The author corrected only explicit outline-duration statements and preserved minimum-480-second requirements, factual numbers and source URLs. Independent reinspection found no operative old 480-second outline instructions in the effective requests. Historical packages and receipts remain unchanged.
2. T28 and T33 initially retained the original eight-minute chapter time ranges in their effective headings. The author removed those stale heading suffixes and rebuilt the manifest. All 92 effective chapter sets now have continuous budgets totaling 600 seconds and no old MM:SS heading ranges. T33's factual UTC and time-offset examples remain unchanged in the chapter content.

## Independent planning and source audit

- 473 unique catalog/ID records: first season 100, second season 92, third season 100, brand stories 100 and AI terms 81. Every current source_record_sha256 was recomputed from its source record; all source-file and package SHA256 values matched the current bytes.
- 307 historical planning-source files were compared byte-for-byte against Git commit `282a7b1160b3a561d8fe89596ff1a0f595c30d65`. All are preserved. The added policy is separately bound below.
- First- and second-season effective requests use flat-explainer and target_minutes=10, stay within the 4000-character premise and 2000-character note limits, and preserve the original premises.
- All 92 second-season proposed chapter budgets sum to 600 seconds. Their chapter content remains present verbatim in the original Markdown after whitespace normalization. Only budget allocations and stale heading timestamps change.
- All 184 second-season Shorts are hash-identical to their original bundles. The 460 effective long-video locale titles are unchanged; long descriptions differ only in the explicit outline-duration digits 480 to 600. Shorts packaging remains in the preserved source bundles.
- The eight duplicate rejections B30/B32/B33/B38/T39/A26/A27/A30 remain absent from the effective adopted catalog. AI term ai-agent remains COVERED_DO_NOT_REMAKE; production lookup rejects it.
- Brand stories retain 780-second targets and the 720–900-second range. AI terms retain a 600-second target and the 540–660-second range. Third-season items remain checked candidates requiring outlines; they do not acquire fabricated one-off inputs or media acceptance.

## Runtime and input review

The QA assemble item now checks the current unwrapped TTS timeline, checked final-frame count and their speech hash. It requires at least 14,400 frames at 30 fps for both body and final cut. One frame below the minimum fails; intro/outro frames cannot make a short body pass. Brand selections must match the body and presentation timeline. Missing or stale proof cannot use target_minutes or an earlier passing checks.json as measured acceptance. Ordinary drama, compilations and Shorts keep their existing rules.

Catalog slugs, the flat-explainer look and explicit knowledge/story categories cover the requested producers. The story worker explicitly emits format=drama and category=story. A missing format or unknown category is already a lint error before QA, so it cannot use classifier fallback as a legal bypass. The final-review push runs QA again for the current final cut.

API and UI source review confirms flat-explainer requests default to 10 minutes and accept only whole minutes in the 8–20 range. Ordinary drama retains the 1–8 range and default 3. Style-only patches preserve the rule about an existing bible, choose the appropriate format default and repair a legacy short explainer when its style is explicitly reselected; unrelated edits can still pause or rename it. This independent review did not execute a browser acceptance run or the API/UI suites.

## PR #1105 category-report increment: 4 files (2026-10-02)

Reviewer: claude-pr-review-1105. Author: claude-pr-1105 (the Claude session that wrote PR #1105, "fix(video): the worker and review-push report the video's category"). The reviewer read the complete delta of the four bound files from origin/main to HEAD d22c1927 (branch claude/video-report-category, which has origin/main merged in) and checked that origin/main's bytes of each file equal the preceding bound hashes, so this delta is exactly the unreviewed change. The other 66 bindings are untouched and still match their current bytes. The reviewer also read the non-bound tools/video/review/sync.mjs and story.test.mjs deltas, and every `category` use under tools/video, to judge duration reach.

Findings: flow.mjs renames recordedVideoId to recorded(), which reads the same video.json and additionally returns `state.category ?? video.category` when it is in VIDEO_CATEGORIES. report() adds that value to the /admin/videos payload only. review-push (sync.mjs) adds video.json's category to its report payload the same way. Neither path writes category into video.json, the automation state or any duration input, and nothing reads the site's category back. The only duration consumer of category, isKnowledgeLongform in core/duration.mjs (knowledge categories ai-terms, explainer, story), still reads only the local doc and keeps its accepted bytes. The new VIDEO_CATEGORIES import sits beside the unchanged minEpisodeMinutes import. The planning-range code, effectiveEpisodeMinutes calls, explainer default 10 and 8–20, drama 1–8 default 3, brand 13 minutes, minEpisodeMinutes floor, 14,400-frame body/final guard and fixture opt-out boundary are byte-unchanged, and there is no new VIDEO_MIN_EPISODE_MINUTES assignment. The test fixtures set category "tutorial", which is not a knowledge category. In automation.test.mjs it is written only after QA, publish and done, before the YouTube-id sync, so no fixture's knowledge/body-guard or drama classification changes. The automated.md line documents only the reported filing field.

Commands: `node tools/video/long-form/cli.mjs check` before rebinding failed only on the four stale bindings, exit 1. The automation, story, review sync, long-form, core duration and QA duration tests ran 148: 147 passed and the one failure was the shipped review-binding test reporting the same four stale bindings. After rebinding, the review test and CLI check are rerun by the reviewer. Non-claims: no CI, browser, real media, provider or PostgreSQL acceptance. This is DURATION_ONLY compatibility review, not acceptance of the category-filing feature itself. Required duration fixes remaining: none.

Reviewer: `claude-pr-review-1112`. Author: `claude-opus-5-5-worker-thumbs` (the Claude session that wrote PR #1112, "the worker draws the language thumbnails before a language batch", branch claude/worker-draws-thumbnails). Scope: DURATION_ONLY. The reviewer did not write these changes.

The reviewer read the full `git diff a775b753 2f456077` of those two files, and the unbound tools/video/render/cli.mjs delta (the new `render --thumbnails-only`) for context. origin/main has since gained #1111 (b11e01eb), which touches no bound file. The other 68 bindings are unchanged.

Findings. flow.mjs adds `LOCALES` to the schema import beside the unchanged `minEpisodeMinutes`, and imports `localizedThumbnailHash`. The new `drawLanguageThumbnails` runs before `captions` and `package` in both language-batch paths. It compares each locale's `localizedThumbnailHash` with the frames manifest's `thumbnail_locales` and, when one is owed, runs `render --slug <slug> --thumbnails-only` (plus `--channel` from VIDEO_BROWSER_CHANNEL). A failed or throwing render is logged as a note and the batch goes on. render's `--thumbnails-only` refuses a manifest whose visual_hash is not the current script's, draws only thumbnails/<locale>.jpg and rewrites the manifest with only `thumbnail_locales`/`thumbnail_locale_gaps` replaced; slide states, scenes, fps, thumbnail.jpg and final.mp4 are left as they are. None of this reads or writes target_minutes, effectiveEpisodeMinutes, minEpisodeMinutes or VIDEO_MIN_EPISODE_MINUTES, frame counts or the 14,400-frame body/final proof. None touches narration lines, the format defaults or ranges (explainer 10 and 8–20, drama 1–8 default 3, brand 13), or the fixture opt-out. In the test file, `finishedVideo` gains an `openRenderer` stand-in and its render stub skips `--thumbnails-only`. Two new tests cover the draw and the not-drawn note. They assert that the manifest gains only the language fields, that final.mp4 and thumbnail.jpg are byte-identical, and that the pipeline steps (frames rendered, video assembled, final approved) are unchanged. Two existing tests gain assertions that render is not run when a locale has no thumbnail words or the video's work files were tidied; the tidied test now translates with #1108's `withThumbnail` helper. No test changes a duration expectation, and no test sets VIDEO_MIN_EPISODE_MINUTES.

Findings. In client.mjs (context), a stage run that was sent and lost its answer throws the new RUN_UNCERTAIN instead of being sent again. That covers a dropped connection, an unreadable answer body, and a 5xx that is not one of the API's settled codes. flow.mjs imports RUN_UNCERTAIN and the sheet-units helpers; the schema import with the unchanged `minEpisodeMinutes` is not touched. `stage()` tags such an error with its stage. `step()`'s catch hands it to the new `unanswered`, which ends the run and blocks the video until the owner retries, instead of paying for the stage again. `translateLocale` now asks a worksheet longer than 24 lines (UNIT_LINES) or 2,400 characters (UNIT_CHARS) in units: a metadata unit, then runs of the lines still to translate. Each unit is translated, then reviewed, and kept in <workdir>/i18n/<locale>.units.json under the hash of what was asked. A unit whose answer leaves a line out or empty is asked again. A review without its own worksheet is now retried instead of merging the unreviewed translation. i18n-merge runs once on the reassembled sheet, and a refused merge drops only the units it names. `assembleSheet` puts back only the text of each line and metadata field; ids, sources and each line's dub budget come from the sheet i18n-sheet wrote. `unitVideo` narrows the scenes a lines unit is shown and does not change the script. The new `unitLimits` caps size one translation request, not a video. None of this reads or writes target_minutes, effectiveEpisodeMinutes, minEpisodeMinutes or VIDEO_MIN_EPISODE_MINUTES, frame counts or the 14,400-frame body/final proof. None touches narration lines (only translation worksheets and their merge), the format defaults or ranges (explainer 10 and 8–20, drama 1–8 default 3, brand 13), or the fixture opt-out. The test file only adds: a `lostAnswer` 504 stand-in and three tests. They cover a writer whose answer is lost (blocked, not resent, asked once more on the owner's retry); a long sheet translated in four units, where a lost unit resumes after the retry and each unit is reviewed once; and a caption review without its worksheet, or with a line left out, never merging the translation. No existing test changes, no test changes a duration expectation, and no test sets VIDEO_MIN_EPISODE_MINUTES; the file's existing fixture opt-out line is byte-unchanged.

Non-claims. The reviewer read the full delta of all three files; no network, no fetch, no implementation file edited.

Findings. prompts.mjs changes one hunk, the slides writer's TEMPLATE_GUIDE: the `shot` bullet now briefs a picture the way a photographer briefs an illustrator (shot size, place and time of day, one concrete action, the object and its material, where the light comes from; no style words; a camera move chosen for the picture and never the move of the shot before, with lint refusing three in a row; the transition left out by default), the cadence paragraph's alternation list reads "a person and a metaphor, a quiet picture after a busy one", and a new "Pictures travel" paragraph asks each chapter for its own place and props, no place or object in more than a third of the shots, and everyday rather than tech metaphors. Findings. The reviewer read the whole diff of both files: 6 added lines each, none removed. There are two new paragraphs and two new reference-table rows. The first paragraph tells agents writing a drama with a cast to read references/drama-craft.md before each episode's script and storyboard. Agents then run `drama_craft_check.mjs` and either fix the rows it misses or answer them in their report. The paragraph says outright that the check "blocks no command and changes no video's length rules" (不擋任何指令，不改任何一種影片的長度規定). It also says the check does not apply to dramas without a cast (explainers, brand stories). The second paragraph points rough-picture feedback to references/visual-quality.md. It adds no per-shot approval and widens no budget, model or publication authority. The table rows only point to these two references, the two scripts and docs/videos/drama-craft/. No added line mentions target_minutes, episode minutes, the 480-second or 14,400-frame body/final floors, the eight-minute non-drama floor, the drama 1–8 range, explainer 10 with 8–20, brand 13 minutes, measured narration/frame proof, VIDEO_MIN_EPISODE_MINUTES or the anime runtime policy, and none changes any of them.

The reviewer read drama-craft.md, the duration-related lines of visual-quality.md, drama_craft_check.mjs and yt_shot_probe.js, and the unbound drama.md, writer-drama.md and animation-production.md deltas for context. All of their numeric targets are inside an episode. They cover shot length (median 2–4 s, best 2.5–3.5; 90th percentile ≤ 6 s; longest ≤ 8 s; spread ≥ 2), how many shots open the episode (≥ 4 in the first 10 s, ≥ 10 in the first 30 s), the first character line within 10 s, line length (median ≤ 12 characters, ≤ 10% over 20), narrator share ≤ 35%, shot-size, motion and dissolve shares, and an opening monologue of 45 s or less. None sets or limits an episode's total length. The unchanged "3-minute episode" cost example now says it means about 50–70 shots, and 3 minutes is still inside the drama 1–8 range. drama-craft.md says to keep shots when the median falls between 2 and 3 s and lint's existing median warning fires. That warning is a lint advisory and has nothing to do with duration floors. The 5-second channel intro is listed only as a pending owner decision, with the default unchanged, and timings are counted from the body's first frame. So the intro is not used to pad or shorten a body. visual-quality.md says cutting is paced by comprehension and not by fixed seconds per shot. It forbids padding with freeze frames or narration and adds no approval step.

drama_craft_check.mjs imports only `readFileSync` and `pathToFileURL`. It writes no file, and it exits non-zero only with `--strict` (1) or for an unreadable file (2). yt_shot_probe.js runs only in a browser tab on a YouTube watch page. A grep of tools/video, package.json, .github and apps/api/app finds no reference to drama_craft_check, drama-craft or yt_shot_probe. The only caller is the new standalone test tools/drama-craft-check.test.mjs. So the craft check does not run in, and cannot block or rewrite, any duration-bound lint, QA, assemble, plan or inputs step.

Baseline: HEAD 04818c3f is one commit past origin/main 873cfc73 (#1164). The commit changes 32 files, 13 of them bound. For each of the 13, the bytes of `git show origin/main:<file>` hash (Node crypto) to the value review.json binds, the working tree equals HEAD, and the other 95 bindings carry their bound hashes, so `git diff origin/main..HEAD -- <file>` is exactly the unreviewed change: tools/video/assemble/cli.mjs +27/−4, automation/flow.mjs +6/−3, automation/prompts.mjs +82/−24, automation/series.mjs +6/−1, automation/series.test.mjs +17/−0, core/drama.mjs +56/−24, core/drama.test.mjs +63/−8, core/lint.mjs +14/−4, core/lint.test.mjs +83/−0, core/schema.mjs +7/−3, core/timeline.mjs +2/−2, core/timeline.test.mjs +1/−1 and media/clips.test.mjs +58/−1. The reviewer read the full delta of all 13 and, for context only, the new unbound tools/video/core/craft.mjs and the unbound deltas of assemble/drama.mjs, media/clips.mjs, media/keyframes.mjs, media/ledger.mjs and docs/videos/DRAMA.md. No network, no fetch, no implementation file edited.

Finding, schema.mjs and timeline.mjs: a duration rule is weakened (required fix below). On origin/main `validateScenes` accepted a scene with `action_seconds` only when `isLongAnime(doc) && validateAnimePolicy(doc).length === 0`, and `buildTimeline` threw on one unless `isLongAnime(doc)`. HEAD computes that `validAnime` unchanged and adds `timedAction = validAnime || hasCast(doc)` (`hasCast` is on origin/main at line 137 with the same definition: format drama with a non-empty `characters`), and `buildTimeline` throws only when neither `isLongAnime(doc)` nor `hasCast(doc)`. The integer 1–8 range, the template, prompt and motion requirement, the empty `lines` rule and the frames a silent shot adds (`action * FPS` into `total_frames` and `actions`, an unchanged loop body) are as before, and the long-anime gate itself (`isLongAnime`, `validateAnimePolicy`, `runtime_spec`, the ±60 s body check and the fit proof in duration.mjs, the `fit` freeze and slow refusal in `validateDrama`) is untouched. What changed is who may declare silent body time, so the reviewer checked which floored documents `hasCast` can reach. The eight-minute floor (`needsMinimumLength`, lint lines 321–324 and qa/cli.mjs, both unchanged): never, because a non-drama format is not `hasCast` and `validateDrama` (drama.mjs line 435, unchanged) refuses characters on the explainer preset. The 480-second knowledge floor is different. `isKnowledgeLongform` (duration.mjs, unchanged) holds for any format-drama document with category ai-terms, explainer or story, or a slug starting sothatswhy-, ai-term- or story-, whatever its cast, and nothing in drama.mjs, schema.mjs, lint.mjs or story.mjs refuses a cast on such a document. A brand story is exactly that: plans.mjs (line 155) binds each of the 100 stories of stories.json, every one with a `story-` slug, with `minimum_content_seconds` 480, `minimum_final_seconds` 480 and `target_range_seconds` [720, 900], and docs/videos/STORY.md lets a story list up to three recurring leads in `characters`; `storyProblems` requires still shots and narrator lines, which a shot with `visual: "still"` and `lines: []` satisfies. Probe (Node 24.13.0, a scratch script outside the repository): the story fixture with slug story-rolling-suitcase (plan A01), one character and one shot { template shot, action_seconds 8, lines [], visual still, a prompt, camera "Wide, locked", a motion } passes `validateVideo` with no error, `lintVideo` with no error and 13 warnings (all craft rows), `storyProblems` with no error, and `estimateTimeline` adds 240 frames as `actions: [{ silent-beat, 292–532 }]`. With 60 such shots the body is 17,723 frames on 3,083 narrated frames (103 s of narration), still with no validation, lint or story error, and `knowledgeDurationProblems` on that body (speech hash matched, checked frames equal to the presented frames, no bookends) returns nothing: the 14,400-frame floor reads as met, and the final-cut floor compares `metrics.frames` with the same timeline, so it reads as met too. The same document without the character fails `validateVideo` on `scenes[1].action_seconds` and `scenes[1].lines` and `buildTimeline` throws, which is what every document outside a valid long-anime policy did before this commit. So for a brand story with leads, and for any knowledge-catalogue drama that lists characters, declared silent time now counts toward the 480-second measured body minimum and the 14,400-frame body and final floors, where before only measured narration and the fixed pauses could. That weakens the rule. The native long-anime increment above admitted silent frames only under `long-anime-v1` with a `runtime_spec`, a measured `timing_basis` and per-shot fit evidence, and this commit's own docs/videos/DRAMA.md says a narrated video has no such shot because its length is measured on its narration; the code's predicate is `hasCast`, which a narrated brand story with leads satisfies. A second consequence, with no floor involved: the long-anime document of drama.test.mjs (built on the cast fixture) with its policy and `runtime_spec` deleted no longer gets the `action_seconds` error and `buildTimeline` no longer throws on it (probed: 1,190 frames with one action); the test "timed action refuses … an ordinary drama" kept passing because its regex was loosened from /complete long-anime production policy/ to /long-anime production policy/, which now matches the unchanged schema error for an anime episode over eight minutes without a policy (schema.mjs line 304), and the "silent action cannot extend an ordinary episode" case of timeline.test.mjs still throws only because the minimal fixture has no characters. Neither test reaches the floored case above.

Required fix (both schema.mjs `validateScenes` and timeline.mjs `buildTimeline`): outside a valid long-anime policy, accept `action_seconds` only on a drama with a cast that carries no duration floor, that is, refuse it when `isKnowledgeLongform(doc)` or `needsMinimumLength(doc)` holds, whatever the cast; put the predicate where both files can import it without a cycle (duration.mjs imports timeline.mjs, which imports schema.mjs). Add a regression: the story fixture with a `story-` slug, one character and a silent still shot must fail `validateVideo` on `.action_seconds` and `.lines` and make `buildTimeline` throw, while the long-anime fixture and the cast fixture (slug fixture-drama, no category) keep passing; and give the drama test's no-policy case back an assertion on the `action_seconds` error. Until then neither file is rebound. The other eleven files are not rebound either: the receipt's fields describe one increment, the fix will change the tests again, and the verdict is on the 13-file increment as a whole. The findings on those eleven follow, so the next increment can rest on them where the bytes are unchanged, after re-hashing.

MAX_SOURCE_CLIP_SECONDS 10 adds an error when `from_s` plus the estimated shot length passes 10 s, which constrains where a cut starts while the shot's own length stays the timeline's; the `source` validation (an earlier clip shot, not itself sourced or a still; no `start_frame`, `end_frame`, still or thumbnail; a drama only) adds errors; `cameraMove` reads `camera` alone and adds "locked", and feeds only `pictureVarietyProblems` for illustrated slides; `isSourced` exempts a cut from the near-identical-prompt warning; `needsMinimumLength`, `isExplainer`, `hasCast`, `validateSeries` and the long-anime freeze and slow refusal are unchanged. lint.mjs: the craft rows are warnings from `craftProblems` on lint's estimated timeline, gated `hasCast(doc) && !isLongAnime(doc)`; craft.mjs reads shot lengths, sizes, moves and lines, returns rows and writes nothing; the eight-minute floor block and the long-anime `runtime_spec` warning are unchanged; `productionShotProblems` adds an error for a sourced shot whose `from_s * FPS` plus its span passes 8 s, beside the unchanged 8-second shot limit; `productionClipProblems` judges a sourced entry on its source clip's `qc.metrics.duration` from the offset, requiring `duration * FPS >= offset + span`, so no less evidence than before. series.mjs `scriptVerdict(check, series, craft = null)`: `craftGateProblems` returns nothing for null or `applies: false` and otherwise the CRAFT_GATE_ROWS misses (hook.*, motion.opening, size.face, size.wide, size.reestablish, size.stall); the coverage beats and the closed-finale list are unchanged. flow.mjs `scriptGate`: the hands-off branch passes `craft` only when `hasCast(video) && !isLongAnime(state)`, so a long anime passes null; `MAX_PROMPT_FIX_ROUNDS` is 2 on both sides and the `rounds < MAX_PROMPT_FIX_ROUNDS` test is unchanged, so craft rows spend the same two rounds and no more; `estimateTimeline` is imported for the craft reading only; The drama writer's "lint refuses more than 12 and warns above 10" matches MAX_SHOT_SECONDS and WARN_SHOT_SECONDS, "lint warns when the median falls under 2 seconds" matches MIN_MEDIAN_SHOT_SECONDS, the production guide's "normally 2–5 seconds, never a shot over 8" sits inside the unchanged 8-second production rule, the discuss variant's "within 2 to 8 seconds" is writer guidance, and the writer's `action_seconds` sentence repeats the schema's and so inherits the finding above. assemble/cli.mjs: `offered(frames, scene) = frames − (scene.from_frame ?? 0)` is applied to the probed clip frames in the anime pre-probe and in the ordinary path before `fitPlan(available, scene.frames, scene.fit)`; `fitPlan` in assemble/drama.mjs is unchanged (available ≥ needed: speed 1 and a trim; otherwise the existing slow or pad rule, which the anime fit proof refuses); `scene.frames` still comes from the timeline, `clipSegmentArgs` trims with `trim=start_frame=<from_frame>` and encodes exactly `scene.frames` frames, so a sourced shot neither shortens nor stretches a measured cut, and a cut whose `from_frame` reaches the clip's end exits with usage before any segment is encoded; the source-frame PSNR check only adds problems; `metrics.frames` is unchanged. The tests: drama.test.mjs asserts that the cast fixture (no floor) accepts `action_seconds` and its narrated copy refuses it, the `cameraMove` cases, the `source` validation and that the fixture's only warnings are craft rows; lint.test.mjs adds the craft warnings, the 2 s and 3 s median thresholds, the silent shot (90 frames for 3 s) on the cast fixture and its refusal on the narrated copy, and the 10 s and 8 s source errors, with its eight-minute floor test unchanged; timeline.test.mjs changes one regex; series.test.mjs shortens the hands-off fixture's lines so the craft rows pass and adds a `scriptVerdict` test (null and `applies: false` add nothing, pace rows do not send back); clips.test.mjs covers the dry run and the run of a sourced shot (nothing bought, a `cut` ledger entry, `needs_review` past the clip's end). None of these changes a frame, minute or floor expectation.

Reviewer: `claude-pr-review-1169-merge`. Baseline: `git show 873cfc732:apps/web/components/admin-video-reviews.test.tsx` hashes to SHA256 `5d8bf674f9f34f97655666e8b61b556a51720f98638c55587e852d167d3cb824`, the value review.json and the table below bound before this increment, and the branch tip `2f54fb90` carries that same blob. HEAD and the working tree hash to `b2eda38830de745b0b000fc26fc7d3e6e17162a408fa5274f4609b15e4b7012c`, the file as it is at `cfeea5d0`. `git diff --stat 873cfc732..cfeea5d03` on the file is 29 insertions, 0 deletions, and `cfeea5d0` touches only this file and the task note, so the whole unreviewed drift is that one diff. Before this section `node tools/video/long-form/cli.mjs check` printed exactly one line, `FAIL: stale duration review binding: apps/web/components/admin-video-reviews.test.tsx` (exit 1), so the other 107 bindings carry their reviewed bytes; I re-hashed the four the diff could have leaned on, admin-video-series.tsx `782f1292…`, admin-video-series.test.tsx `1fa4a70e…`, admin-video-explainer-duration.test.tsx `bb888f17…` and zh-TW/admin.json `aeef4895…`, and each equals its review.json row.

Findings. Three verifiers read the change before me through an inventory lens, a clock lens and a refutation lens; I re-read the whole diff, the current file in full, the drama request form and the week rule in admin-video-series.tsx, and the vitest the branch declares, and I agree with all three. In my own words:

1. What the diff is. Three hunks, nothing removed or edited: lines 93–94 add `vi.useRealTimers()` with a comment to the file-level `afterEach`; lines 248–250 add a comment and `vi.setSystemTime(new Date("2026-09-26T07:00:00Z"))` at the head of the existing test "queues a drama episode from the form, lists the requests and withdraws a queued one"; lines 309–331 add one test, "keeps a queued or started request however old, and drops a finished or withdrawn one a week after it was filed", which pins 2026-10-10T00:00:00Z, answers `GET /drama-requests` with five rows and asserts that the queue lists three of them. `it` blocks go 36 → 37; no `.skip`, `.only` or `.todo` anywhere in the file.

2. Inventory, or what the diff leaves alone. The file's one duration-rule assertion, `getByRole("spinbutton", { name: "長度（分鐘：漫劇 1–8，解說 8–20）" })`, moved from line 284 to line 289 and is byte-identical (the two lines hash alike, and the file contains no 8–12); the same test still posts `target_minutes: 2` for an ink-wash drama (line 298). Every other minute, second and frame assertion lies outside the three hunks and is unchanged: the 9.5-second storyboard shot (241), the 96-second clip (568), the 6-second native action and the refused 9-second one (640, 662), the QA 21 s and 1800 frames (904, 906), the measured anime body of 1314.5 s against the 1320 s target and 1260–1380 s acceptance, 18 s of OP/ED under the 180 s budget, the 1332.5 s final cut, the 1800 s slot and 300 s reserve (938–943) and the zero-budget variants (950–952, 964–966), the nine non-current proofs that keep approval disabled, among them 30000 body frames and a 1200 s earlier spec (993–1016), the 1259 s / 37770-frame, 181 s and 1560 s rejections (1020–1032), the publish, script and audio cards (1044, 1056–1061), the 9.5-minute upload package (1111) and the seven-day review-store notice read against a real `Date.now()` (1353–1357). The file never asserted the 480-second / 14,400-frame floors, the eight-minute floor on a non-drama, the brand story's 720–900 s or plans.json, so this change could not have moved them here.

3. The clock. In vitest 5.0.2, the `^5.0.2` apps/web declares (dist/chunks/index.DGdajAO2.js lines 6854–6868), `setSystemTime` without fake timers installs a clock that fakes only `Date` and `Temporal`, so `setTimeout`, `setInterval`, `waitFor` and the `REFRESH_MS` poll stay real; `useRealTimers` (lines 6810–6819) uninstalls that clock, and the new `afterEach` runs it after every test. Nothing a duration assertion reads looks at the clock: the label is the static admin.json string `targetMinutes` (line 2318) rendered by admin-video-series.tsx line 411, the input's `min`/`max` and the submit guard (lines 411, 415) read `DRAMA_MINUTES` { 1, 8, default 3 } and `EXPLAINER_MINUTES` { 8, 20, default 10 } (lines 85–86), and `NewDramaForm` (lines 362–418) has no `Date` in it. The file's only `Date` use is the week filter at lines 489–490: a request without a `series_slug` is kept while it is queued or started, otherwise only while `Date.parse(created_at) > Date.now() − 7 days`. That is the admin page's list-retention rule (its comment: finished and withdrawn ones drop off after a week); it reads no `target_minutes` and feeds nothing the worker, QA, plans or the API validators consume, so it is not a duration rule. The pin at line 250 sits one to three hours after the fixtures it governs (filed 04:00–06:00Z, withdrawn 06:30Z), far from the seven-day edge, and only brings the withdrawn row back for the unchanged `已取消` assertion at line 305, which is exactly what went red on the 2026-10-03 wall clock. The pinned `Date` cannot leak: vitest isolates each file in its own worker (the run below spawned one per file), the sibling admin-video-series.test.tsx restores timers in its own `afterEach` (line 169) and still pins default 3, max 8 and the 10 / 8 / 20 explainer switch (lines 401–406, 411), admin-video-explainer-duration.test.tsx still pins `["3", "1", "8"]` and `["10", "8", "20"]` (lines 41–46), and the real-clock test at lines 1353–1357 runs after both pinned tests in file order and passed.

4. Refutation. The new fixtures' `target_minutes: 3` (line 311) is the drama default inside 1–8; `DramaQueue` only interpolates it into the `{minutes} 分鐘` label (admin-video-series.tsx line 447, admin.json line 2330), the mock answers `GET` alone, the form is never opened and no `POST` or API validator runs, so the value reaches no range check and the test asserts nothing about length. The new test probes the seven-day edge at one minute on either side (a `done` row filed 2026-10-03T00:01Z kept; `done` and `cancelled` rows filed 2026-10-02T23:59Z dropped; `queued` and `started` rows from 2026-09-20 kept) and only adds assertions. The pre-existing fake-timer test (lines 800–833) already restored real timers in its own `finally`, so the added `afterEach` call is idempotent there. The verifiers' mutation checks are theirs, not mine: with the zh-TW label changed to 8–12 only line 289 fails, and with the week predicate replaced by `true` only the new test fails, with 5 rows instead of 3 (the author's task note records the same for the predicate).

## PR #1172 print-looks increment: 8 files (2026-10-03)

Reviewer: `claude-pr-review-1172`. Scope: DURATION_ONLY. The reviewer did not write these changes and edited only this report and review.json.

Baseline: the merge base of HEAD with origin/main (5af4ffeb, #1168) is cfeea5d0 (#1169), and the one commit on the branch past it is 05b9bafd. The receipt before this increment is the one the "PR #1160 merge with main" section above bound at 3bc7f343; the two commits between it and cfeea5d0 are 873cfc73 (#1164, apps/web/package.json and package-lock.json only) and cfeea5d0 itself, which changes apps/web/components/admin-video-reviews.test.tsx and a tasks/ file. So at cfeea5d0 the bytes of every bound file but that test equal the hashes bound before this increment (checked for all 108 by hashing the cfeea5d0 blobs), and `git diff cfeea5d0 HEAD` of the seven bound files the branch changes is exactly the unreviewed change: automated.md +1/−1, automation.test.mjs +16/−3, flow.mjs +6/−5, prompts.mjs +13/−4, drama.mjs +79/−10, drama.test.mjs +72/−8, look-keyframes.test.mjs +99/−10. Of the 23 files that range changes, only these seven are bound. The eighth stale binding, admin-video-reviews.test.tsx, is #1169's: cfeea5d0~1's bytes hash to 5d8bf674…3cb824, the value bound before this increment, and cfeea5d0, HEAD, the working tree and origin/main all hold b2eda388…b7012c, so `git show cfeea5d0` (+29/−0) is exactly its unreviewed change; the branch does not touch the file. The working tree equals HEAD for all 108 bound files. The reviewer read the full delta of all eight files; no network, no fetch, no implementation file edited.

Findings, the seven. automated.md changes one line, the 插圖投影片 bullet, and in it only the `look` parenthetical: the default is no longer "預設 tech-story" but, when the writer names none, one of five print presets the worker picks by slug, with one style plate drawn per video as every picture's reference (ILLUSTRATED.md §第二輪). The bullet's pacing clause (每 5–8 秒換一張畫面、卡片狀態 ≤8 秒、插圖至少佔一半時間、開場 20 秒內落鉤, one or two sentences per shot, the 600 ms dissolve beat) is byte-unchanged in the same line, and every other line of the file, the ones naming minutes included, is byte-identical. flow.mjs changes its drama.mjs import (slidesPresetFor for SLIDES_PRESET) and, in `settle`, the illustrated branch's default look, `{ preset: slidesPresetFor(slug), ...(video.look ?? {}) }` for `{ preset: SLIDES_PRESET, ... }`, with the comment above it; `slug` was already a parameter of settle, and the music and sfx defaults below are unchanged context. The `effectiveEpisodeMinutes` import and its uses are byte-identical (four mentions before and after), and no changed line reads target_minutes, minEpisodeMinutes or a frame count. prompts.mjs changes two hunks of the slides writer's prompt: in TEMPLATE_GUIDE a new "Pictures live in a day" paragraph (at most half the pictures at night or under a lamp, which lint counts; in every chapter two or three people acting on each other and a close-up of a thing in hands; an occasional small joke) and the `look` sentence, which now tells the writer to leave look out so the worker picks one of the channel's print looks (riso-teal, riso-navy, riso-forest, riso-plum, linocut-teal) and to name one only when the topic calls for it; and in the writer's video.json field list, `"look" left out unless one of the channel's print looks suits the topic`. The sentence "A shot carries one or two sentences, 5 to 8 seconds" and the cadence paragraph are outside both hunks, and the file's 12 target_minutes lines are byte-identical. drama.mjs adds PRINT_NEGATIVE, RISO_INKS and `risoPresets()`, which build the four riso-* presets, and spreads them with a `linocut-teal` preset into PRESETS before `custom` (each a style, a negative and the same "slow push in or gentle drift" motion as tech-story); exports SLIDES_PRESETS (the five, in rotation order) and makes SLIDES_PRESET its first, "riso-teal" instead of "tech-story"; adds `slidesPresetFor(slug)`, an FNV-1a hash of the slug modulo five; widens SHOT_SIZE (built from SIZE_WORD: the usual spellings, and a size only at the start of the prompt or followed by shot, view, angle or of), LOOK_WORDS (the print techniques, more dairy exclusions for "cream") and PROMPT_STOPWORDS; adds DARK_LIGHT and the exported DARK_SHARE_WARN = 1/2; folds more plurals in `motifOf`; and in `pictureVarietyProblems` names `data.motion` instead of `data.camera` in the same-move error when the move came from the motion prompt, and warns, from MOTIF_MIN_SHOTS pictures up, when more than half are at night or under a lamp. Every one of those counts shots or a share of shots, or shapes a prompt string; none counts seconds, minutes or frames ("frame" in PRINT_NEGATIVE, the preset styles and the comments is the picture's frame). `pictureVarietyProblems` still returns nothing unless `illustrated(doc)`; `shotProblems`, `needsMinimumLength`, `validateDrama`, `validateSeries`, `illustrated` and `isDrama` are byte-identical from `export function` to the closing brace (compared between cfeea5d0 and HEAD), as are the lines carrying FPS, MAX_SHOT_SECONDS, WARN_SHOT_SECONDS, MIN_MEDIAN_SHOT_SECONDS, LONG_SHOT_SHARE_WARN, SAME_MOVE_RUN_MAX, MOTIF_SHARE_WARN and MOTIF_MIN_SHOTS. SLIDES_PRESET's only other reader is the `validateDrama` message for an illustrated video with no look, whose line is unchanged. The new default reaches lookHash through settle, so an illustrated video with no look of its own would be redrawn once; that is a picture-cache matter, not a length. automation.test.mjs makes the drama settle test async and adds to it: an illustrated fixture with its look deleted settles to `slidesPresetFor(slug)` for two slugs, both in SLIDES_PRESETS, and a fixture that names tech-story keeps it; the `bible` fixture line loses a space; and the illustrated settle test expects `{ preset: slidesPresetFor("y") }` for `{ preset: "tech-story" }`. The fixture opt-out `process.env.VIDEO_MIN_EPISODE_MINUTES ??= "0"` (line 32) is byte-unchanged and no duration expectation changes. drama.test.mjs rewrites the printmaker test's preset assertions for the five SLIDES_PRESETS (the style's opening, dot eyes, the centre third, "runs past all four edges", no "full-bleed", the negative's words, style ≤ 600 and negative ≤ 400 characters, five distinct styles), asserts `slidesPresetFor` is stable and spreads six slugs over at least four looks, keeps the tech-story assertions that still hold, and adds one test of the lint: sizes read as sizes and not as "a medium bowl", dairy not read as the palette, plurals folded (ferry/ferries in four of eleven is over a third, shelf and box in three are not; shoes, series and species kept), the same-move error's path and message for a motion prompt and for a camera, and the dark-light warning at 4 of 6 pictures, not at 3 of 6, and not under six. The `estimateTimeline` import is unchanged context for the existing shotProblems call; the new lines assert no frame count, length or minute, and the 600 and 400 they check are character limits of the preset strings. look-keyframes.test.mjs imports STYLE_PLATE_ID and STYLE_PLATE_PROMPT, adds a readJson helper, and in the illustrated test expects one style plate before the five shots: the dry-run lines ("after one style plate", "references: style plate", US$0.46 for one take and US$1.39 at 3 takes for six pictures at 0.067 + 0.01 each, where it said 0.39 and 1.16 for five), the plate's prompt, purpose, empty references, plate.json and its upload, every shot's `{ sha256, role: "style" }` reference, the judge's "style plate" file and question, manifest.plate, the ledger at shots + 1, a kept plate on a card edit, the plate outliving a camera edit, a new plate and every picture on a new look, and no plate when the look has its own style_frames; the craft and clean rubric questions' new wording and every rubric question ≤ 400 characters; and a new test of the paper-margin trim (trimmed copies in plate.json and the manifest with their margins, the judge sent the untrimmed picture, a rerun keeping the trimmed copies). The prices are money per picture, the counts are pictures, and the fixture opt-out on line 19 is byte-unchanged; no test changes a duration expectation. Across the seven files, a scan of the changed lines finds no target_minutes, effectiveEpisodeMinutes, minEpisodeMinutes, MIN_EPISODE_MINUTES, VIDEO_MIN_EPISODE_MINUTES, needsMinimumLength, minute, 480, 780, 14,400, runtime_spec, action_seconds, target_seconds or `fit`, and none sets the explainer's 10-minute default or 8–20 range, the drama's 1–8 range or the brand story's 13.

Author: `claude-opus-5-5` (the Claude session that wrote commit `962161c4d95a41d2197d109b7b2d88bf0bf4d41e`, "narration takes made stale by the accent change are recorded again, not refused for ever", branch claude/tts-stale-takes-resynth, task `2026-10-03-video-worker-narration-takes-made-stale`). Scope: DURATION_ONLY for the three changed bindings below. The reviewer did not write these changes, ran no git command that writes, and edited only this report and review.json.

Baseline: the commit's parent is main `b09819feffe2aa6080895a3b986537e8222991d9` (#1175). All 108 registered files' bytes at that parent hash exactly to the preceding receipt (each compared with review.json by script), and the preceding report's SHA256 is `12b59c11948f36a5ce3e18e22e7f5bd43102d2c7f078a25dd63ad931dad3fb73`; every paragraph before the binding table is preserved byte for byte. Of the commit's six changed files, three are bound: tools/video/automation/flow.mjs (+6/−1), tools/video/automation/automation.test.mjs (+21/−0) and tools/video/tts/tts.test.mjs (+6/−0). The registry remains exactly 108 paths, with these three hashes rebound and the other 105 unchanged: before rebinding, `node tools/video/long-form/cli.mjs check` failed on these three paths and nothing else. The reviewer read the full delta of all three and, for context, the unbound new tools/video/tts/takes.mjs (SHA256 `b76cb76ab26e6f4d46930eab5f305a1b50f5015ad30e5aabf06fbb14f542ae48`), the unbound delta of tools/video/tts/cli.mjs (SHA256 `36330d150e4b50768b4db25a670b17b0e72ae6f5735b945478552336c10927ee`) and the new task file; no network, no fetch, no implementation file edited.

Findings. Per file:

- flow.mjs: one import (`staleTakes` from ../tts/takes.mjs), a four-line comment and one changed expression in the worker's "narration synthesized" step. `refresh` was `sameScript && !previous.audio_evidence`; it now also requires `!staleTakes(project.doc, project.lexicon, workdir).length`. The only effect is which arguments the same `tts` command gets: with an unchanged script, no audio evidence and at least one saved take whose key or WAV is no longer current, the worker runs `tts --slug <slug>` instead of `tts --slug <slug> --refresh-evidence`. The comment two lines above the import ("Slides keep the general eight-minute floor…") and every line that names target_minutes, minEpisodeMinutes, effectiveEpisodeMinutes, MIN_EPISODE or needsMinimumLength are outside the hunks and byte-identical; the block on audio evidence that no longer matches its takes (the line before the change) is unchanged.
- automation.test.mjs: 21 lines appended to the end of the illustrated-slides walk. They strip `audio_evidence` and each line's `audio_sha256` from the fixture timeline and assert the worker runs a plain `tts` while the cache carries no current key, then `tts --refresh-evidence` once cache.json holds every line's current key. No existing assertion changes, and the fixture opt-out `process.env.VIDEO_MIN_EPISODE_MINUTES ??= "0"` is outside the hunk.
- tts.test.mjs: one import and five lines inside the existing legacy-evidence test: `staleTakes` returns `[]` while every take is current and the forgotten line's id once the cache drops it. The existing `--refresh-evidence` refusal assertion (`/never synthesizes/`) is unchanged, as is the comment "The fixture videos run seconds; the eight-minute floor has tests of its own."

Unbound context. takes.mjs holds `readCache`, `takeCurrent` and `staleTakes`. `takeCurrent` is the predicate cli.mjs had inline (the line's own key or its request's key in cache.lines, and the WAV on disk), now with `cache.lines?.` and without the `!values.force` term, which cli.mjs keeps at its call site; `tts` therefore decides what to synthesize exactly as before. `staleTakes` applies it to `planRequests(doc, lexicon)` minus the `audio_ref` repeats, the same set `tts` calls `requests`, so the worker's answer and the `pending` test behind the refresh refusal (cli.mjs line 269) agree.

Indirect effects on the duration rules, traced in the current tree. A plain `tts` is the command the worker already ran whenever the script changed. It starts with `lintProject` and exits on any lint error, so the estimated eight-minute floor (lint.mjs) still stands in front of any recording; it measures every clip from the WAVs on disk, rebuilds timeline.json with `buildTimeline` and binds it with `bindAudioEvidence`, so measured-duration evidence is produced by the same code and is never copied from the older timeline. The newly written timeline makes the listening approval stale (approvals.mjs compares the approved file's SHA256), so "narration approved" is asked again, and state.mjs's `assembledSound` requires checks.narration_sha256 to equal the new evidence, so the cut is assembled and measured again: `knowledgeDurationProblems` (480 s, 14,400 frames at 30 fps) and the measured `minMinutes` floor in qa/checks.mjs apply to the new takes. Re-recorded takes can run a different length from the old ones; that length is measured and held to the same floors, which is stricter than a refresh that keeps the old audio. No path lets a video skip lint, the measured timeline, the 480-second check or the QA floor.

A scan of the 33 added lines and one removed line across the three bound files finds no minute, target_minutes, MIN_EPISODE, VIDEO_MIN_EPISODE_MINUTES, needsMinimumLength, 480, 600, 780, 14,400, runtime_spec, action_seconds, target_seconds, frame, duration, seconds, covered, plans.json or source-hash token. So the change touches none of the explainer's 10-minute default or 8–20 range, the drama's 1–8 range, the brand story's 13 minutes, the eight-minute floor, the 480-second / 14,400-frame QA floor, the long-anime policy, measured-duration evidence or docs/videos/long-form/plans.json.

Ran (Node 24.13.0, offline, Windows): before rebinding, `node tools/video/long-form/cli.mjs check` printed FAIL on exactly the three stale bindings above. `node --test tools/video/automation/automation.test.mjs tools/video/tts/tts.test.mjs` ran 94 tests: 94 passed, zero failed, cancelled or skipped, exit 0. The CLI check and tools/video/long-form/review.test.mjs are rerun after rebinding; their results are in the hand-off so this report's hash stays stable.

Non-claims. This review does not accept the decision to re-record rather than keep the older takes, the synthesis cost of doing so, a real provider call or a listening test, the seven stuck videos on the production host, CI, or the three unbound files beyond the reading above. Two cases are left as they were and are not certified here: a timeline that has `audio_evidence` which no longer matches its takes still blocks for a person, and a partly stale legacy video whose `audio_ref` source is still current but has no saved WAV SHA256 in cache.json is refused by `tts` with a usage error either way. PASS is DURATION_ONLY for the three rebound hashes below.

Verdict: PASS — DURATION_ONLY; required duration fixes remaining: none.

## Branch clips-import increment: 2 files (2026-10-04)

Reviewer: `claude-pr-review-clips-import`. Author: `claude-fable-5-1-clips-import` (the Claude session that wrote commit bb73782f, "feat(video): clips import brings a clip made outside the pipeline into a shot", branch claude/kling-hailuoai-video-integration-3b73d6, HEAD bb73782f). Scope: DURATION_ONLY. The reviewer did not write these changes and edited only this report and review.json.

Baseline: the merge base of HEAD with the local origin/main ref is bbd6ae77 (#1180), and the one commit on the branch past it is bb73782f. `git diff --name-only bbd6ae77 HEAD` lists 23 files; intersected in Node with the 108 paths of REVIEW_FILES, two of them are bound: tools/video/core/state.mjs and tools/video/media/clips.test.mjs. The bytes of `git show bbd6ae77:<path>` hash (SHA-256 in Node, the method of review.mjs) to the values review.json bound before this increment, 2b7ed5fc…33fe82 for state.mjs and 9e652b33…3c207b for clips.test.mjs, so `git diff bbd6ae77 HEAD` of the two files is exactly the unreviewed change: state.mjs +11/−0 (33,095 → 33,976 bytes) and clips.test.mjs +216/−1 (45,138 → 63,110 bytes). The working tree equals HEAD for both, the other 106 bindings equal their working-tree bytes, and this report hashed to the receipt's report_sha256 before this section. The reviewer read the full delta of both files; no network, no fetch, no implementation file edited.

Findings, state.mjs. Two hunks, nothing removed. The first adds, after `needsReview`, a module-private `importedClips(manifest)` under a one-line comment: over the manifest's shots that have a file and are neither a still nor a cut from another shot's clip, it counts those carrying `imported_at` per `provider` and returns undefined when there are none, else one string, "N of M clips imported: provider n, …". It reads file, still, source, imported_at and provider, and no seconds, frames, timeline or policy. The second adds one key, `detail`, under a comment, to the "clips generated" entry of `pipelineStatus`: `importedClips(clips)` when the manifest's speech_hash, visual_hash and look_hash are the current ones, else undefined. The entry's `done`, `note` and `todo` lines are unchanged context, so what finishes the step is what finished it before (a current manifest, no needs_review, no productionClipProblems); `detail` enters no `done`, and `next` is still the first step not done. A grep for `step.detail` across tools/, ops/, apps/web/components, apps/api/app and the animation-production scripts finds one reader of a pipeline step, `cmdStatus` in tools/video/cli.mjs (unbound, same commit), which prints it in parentheses after the step's line; the hits in tools/video/templates/templates.mjs and apps/web/components/admin-video-youtube.tsx are a slide template's and a YouTube sync run's own step lists. `checklistFrom` (tools/video/review/sync.mjs), through which flow.mjs, review sync and ops/video/import_outline_reviews.mjs report the steps to the site, maps only id and done, so `detail` is not sent there. The anime lines (hasAnimePolicy, runtimePolicyHash, timingCurrent, animeBodyDurationProblems, animeRuntimeProof, runtimeCurrent; lines 409–420 at HEAD) and the "narration synthesized", "narration approved", "video assembled", "final video approved" and "upload package" entries that read them are outside both hunks. A case-insensitive scan for minute, MIN_EPISODE, needsMinimumLength, 480, 14,400, 600, 780, runtime, target_seconds, target_minutes, action_seconds, anime, policy, freeze, fit, duration, measured, timing_basis, covered, source_hash, plans.json, bodyProblems and audioProblems finds the same 31 lines in the file at bbd6ae77 and at HEAD, and none of the 11 added lines.

Findings, clips.test.mjs. Two hunks. The one removed line is the `./ledger.mjs` import (line 15), which comes back with `importedTotals` added. The other hunk appends 215 lines after the file's last test: three helpers and three tests, taking the file from 17 tests to 20. Lines 1–654 are otherwise byte-identical at bbd6ae77 and at HEAD (a diff of the two revisions' first 654 lines without the ledger import is empty), so no existing test, fixture, helper or expectation changes. The fixture opt-out `process.env.VIDEO_MIN_EPISODE_MINUTES ??= "0"` is line 20 at both revisions, under the same comment ("The fixture videos run seconds; the eight-minute floor has tests of its own."), and is the file's one mention of the variable: it is not new, and no added line reads or sets process.env or carries skip, todo or only. The helpers: `outsideQc(seconds = 8, psnr = 40)` returns a `clipQc` stub, the test seam clips.mjs and four lines of this file already had at the merge base, answering with the existing `goodProbe(seconds)` (h264, 1920x1080, 30 fps), no black interval, freeze or cut, and the given keyframe PSNR; `outsideFile` writes a fake mp4 under the sandbox's base directory; `clipsStep` reads the "clips generated" step from `pipelineStatus`. The first test walks the usage errors (the provider list, the required flags, an unknown shot, a missing file, a --credits that is not a number) and the storyboard gate (EXIT.owner, nothing copied), then one import: the manifest's three hashes and clips_hash; the shot's file, sha256, seconds 8, frames 8 × FPS, `needed_s` (the shot's frames in the fixture's timeline over FPS), first_frame, no judge and needs_review false; provider, plan, credits and imported_at; the take; the ledger entry (status "imported", cost_usd 0), its totals and `importedTotals`; the run record; the same file imported twice keeping one take and one entry; the status detail and its printed line; a dry run that prices the three other shots with the existing `clipSeconds` and not the imported one (four under --force); and the stage keeping the imported clip while it buys the other three. The second: a 5-second clip whose first frame scores 15 dB against its keyframe is left needs_review (EXIT.lint) and booked once, the step not done under its existing note; --force with --note keeps it (needs_review false, forced true, the failed qc still on record) and the step is done; --judge uploads the clip and the cast's sheets and asks the clip rubric, and --usd prices the credits; a judge's refusal exits EXIT.lint with the judge's problem on the shot, and credits are null when none were given. The third: a shot cut from another shot's clip is refused in favour of its source; importing the source moves the cut to the new file (from_frame 30); a second, 1-second import takes the next number, prints "the clip is shorter than its lines" and leaves the cut for a fix (EXIT.lint); a stale timeline and a still are refused (EXIT.usage), and a series with an approved production profile is refused with EXIT.owner and no clips directory. Every seconds, frames and clip_seconds value these tests name is one clip's probed length, one shot's length read from the fixture's timeline, or the ledger's clip seconds and money; none is an episode's or a video's length, and none asserts a floor, a target or a range. The scan above matches one of the 216 added lines, `outsideQc`, for the stub's empty `freezes` list, which is an input of the clip check and not a shot's `fit`. What --force overrides in the second test is a clip's keyframe match, a picture gate. The production-profile refusal is asserted, and the production tests earlier in the file (the eight-second shot limit, the slightly short take refused instead of padded, native 1080p, cached evidence checked on resume) are inside the unchanged lines. So neither file alters, weakens or bypasses a rule recorded in this report: the explainer's 10-minute default and 8–20 range, the drama's 1–8 minutes, the brand story's 13, the eight-minute floor on every video but a drama, the 480-second / 14,400-frame floors, target_minutes, MIN_EPISODE and VIDEO_MIN_EPISODE_MINUTES, the long-anime policy (runtime_spec, action_seconds), measured-runtime evidence, plans.json, the source hashes and the covered status stand as the sections above left them.

Ran (Node 24.13.0, offline, in this worktree at bb73782f): the baseline hashing and the scan above, as scripts. Before rebinding, `node tools/video/long-form/cli.mjs check` printed `FAIL: stale duration review binding: tools/video/core/state.mjs; stale duration review binding: tools/video/media/clips.test.mjs` and exited 1, failing on nothing else. `node --test tools/video/long-form/review.test.mjs tools/video/long-form/plans.test.mjs` ran 7 tests: 6 passed and 1 failed, the shipped binding regression on the same two paths. `node --test tools/video/media/clips.test.mjs` on the branch's bytes: 20 tests, 20 passed, 0 failed, exit 0. `node --test tools/video/core/state.test.mjs tools/video/core/duration.test.mjs tools/video/qa/duration.test.mjs`: 33 tests, 33 passed, exit 0. After rebinding the two rows below and the receipt (author `claude-fable-5-1-clips-import`, reviewer `claude-pr-review-clips-import`, checked 2026-10-04, the two hashes and the report hash computed by a script, never typed), the CLI check and the two long-form test files are run again; their results are in the hand-off of this increment, not in this text, since the report hash covers it.

## Branch clips-import merge with #1182 follow-up: 3 files (2026-10-04)

Findings. Unlike the preceding increments, this one changes a duration rule on purpose. The change is exactly that and no more:

A drama, a brand story (preset "custom"), a long anime (preset "anime-2d") and any document carrying a compilation are not `needsMinimumLength`, so they warn on both sides as before. What was removed was a warning, never an error: no worker flow reads it (flow.mjs and story.mjs feed back lint errors only), so no video that was blocked before passes now and none that passed is blocked.
- lint.test.mjs: one new test of 25 lines, no existing assertion changed. The slides planner and writer and the explainer's common block and two planners replace "the whole within target_minutes" with "at least 8 minutes, aimed at the target, over it is fine; never cut a sourced fact to fit and never pad". Every "never under 8 minutes" stays or is added. The drama planner's "within target_minutes" (line 475) is untouched.
- SKILL.md and its .claude copy (byte-identical, same SHA256): the route table says 「8 分鐘以上（目標 8–12 分鐘，超過上緣沒關係）」 and hard rule 10 gains one sentence, 「沒有上限（站主 2026-10-04 定）」; the rule's first sentence (8 minutes or more for everything but a drama) is unchanged.

The sentences on the 8-minute errors, the measured `assemble` floor, the 10- and 13-minute production targets, the 480-second checks and `VIDEO_MIN_EPISODE_MINUTES` are byte-identical.
- docs/videos/DESIGN.md and README.md: one table cell each. Both keep "at least 8 minutes" and add that 8–12 is the default aim and the upper end is not a limit; README's 10-minute, 8–20, 13-minute, 480-second, 2–4-minute and 30–480-minute statements are byte-identical.

Findings, look-keyframes.test.mjs. Two hunks. The one line changed inside the old file is line 14, the import from ./keyframes.mjs, which gains `bestTake` and `entryStands`; the other 805 of the merge base's 806 lines are byte-identical at the head (compared line by line by script), so no existing test, fixture, helper or expectation changes. The first new test is a unit test of two functions over hand-made objects (a seed, a cache-key string, a judge's overall and passed, a stamp string). `bestTake` returns the latest take that passed, else the one scored highest with the earlier seed on a tie, and null for no take; `entryStands` holds only while every take's key and stamp are the ones that would be asked for now and the end frame's key is the same, an unstamped verdict standing only on a shot that passed.
